<div align="left" data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm"><table class="_Invisible"><col width="602.51px"/><tr height="24.00px"><td><p class="Note">Note:<span style="padding-right:33pt ;">​</span>In this final phase,<span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel,_tinyaes_newmodel,_tinyaes_xcm"> swapping is applied to tag data read from <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_DOUTR register</span>.</p></td></tr></table></div>


When transiting from the header phase to the final phase, the AES peripheral must not be disabled, otherwise the result is wrong.

Application must mask the authentication tag output with tag length to obtain a valid tag.

#### Suspend/resume operations in CCM mode

To suspend the processing of a message in header or payload phase, proceed as follows:

1. If DMA is used, stop the AES DMA transfers to the IN FIFO by clearing the DMAINEN bit of the AES\_CR register. If DMA is not used, make sure that the current computation is completed, which is indicated by the CCF flag of the AES\_SR register set to 1.
2. In the payload phase, if DMA is not used, read four times the AES\_DOUTR register to save the last-processed block. If DMA is used, wait until the CCF flag is set in the AES\_SR register then stop the DMA transfers from the OUT FIFO by clearing the DMAOUTEN bit of the AES\_CR register.
3. Clear the CCF flag of the AES\_SR register, by setting to 1 the CCFC bit of the AES\_CR register.
4. Save the AES\_SUSPxR registers (where x is from 0 to 7) in the memory.
5. Save the AES\_IVRx registers as, during the data processing, they changed from their initial values.
6. Disable the AES peripheral, by clearing the EN bit of the AES\_CR register.
7. Save the current AES configuration in the memory, excluding the initialization vector registers AES\_IVRx. Key registers do not need to be saved as the original key value is known by the application.
8. If DMA is used, save the DMA controller status (pointers for IN data transfers, number of remaining bytes, and so on). In the payload phase, pointers for OUT data transfers must also be saved.

To resume the processing of a message, proceed as follows:

1. If DMA is used, configure the DMA controller in order to complete the rest of the FIFO IN transfers. In the payload phase, the rest of the FIFO OUT transfers must also be configured in the DMA controller.
2. Disable the AES peripheral by clearing the EN bit of the AES\_CR register.
3. Write the suspend register values, previously saved in the memory, back into their corresponding AES\_SUSPxR registers (where x is from 0 to 7).
4. Write the initialization vector register values, previously saved in the memory, back into their corresponding AES\_IVRx registers.
5. Restore the initial setting values in the AES\_CR and AES\_KEYRx registers.
6. Enable the AES peripheral by setting the EN bit of the AES\_CR register.
7. If DMA is used, enable AES DMA requests by setting to 1 the DMAINEN bit (and DMAOUTEN bit if in payload phase) of the AES\_CR register.
<a id="XREF_71598__1_3_13_AES_data_registers_and_data_swapping8"></a>

### 27.4.13 AES data registers and data swapping

#### Data input and output

A 128-bit data block is entered into the AES peripheral with four successive 32-bit word writes into the AES\_DINR register (bitfield DIN[31:0]), the most significant word (bits [127:96]) first, the least significant word (bits [31:0]) last.

A 128-bit data block is retrieved from the AES peripheral with four successive 32-bit word reads from the AES\_DOUTR register (bitfield DOUT[31:0]), the most significant word (bits [127:96]) first, the least significant word (bits [31:0]) last.

The 32-bit data word for AES\_DINR register or from AES\_DOUTR register is organized in big endian order, that is:

- the most significant byte of a word to write into AES\_DINR must be put on the lowest address out of the four adjacent memory locations keeping the word to write, or
- the most significant byte of a word read from AES\_DOUTR goes to the lowest address out of the four adjacent memory locations receiving the word

For using DMA for input data block write into AES, the four words of the input block must be stored in the memory consecutively and in big-endian order, that is, the most significant word on the lowest address. See [Section 27.4.16: AES DMA interface](#XREF_62006__1_3_16_AES_DMA_interface8).

#### Data swapping

The AES peripheral can be configured to perform a bit-, a byte-, a half-word-, or no swapping on the input data word in the AES\_DINR register, before loading it to the AES processing core, and on the data output from the AES processing core, before sending it to the AES\_DOUTR register. The choice depends on the type of data. For example, a byte swapping is used for an ASCII text stream.

The data swap type is selected through the DATATYPE[1:0] bitfield of the AES\_CR register. The selection applies both to the input and the output of the AES core.

For different data swap types, [Figure 206](#XREF_13001__Figure_2__128-bit_block_construction_according_to_the_data_type8) shows the construction of AES processing core input buffer data P127 to P0, from the input data entered through the AES\_DINR register, or the construction of the output data available through the AES\_DOUTR register, from the AES processing core output buffer data P127 to P0.

<a id="XREF_13001__Figure_2__128-bit_block_construction_according_to_the_data_type8"></a>

#### Figure 206. 128-bit block construction with respect to data swap![](MSv42153V2.svg)

**Note:**

The data in AES key registers (AES\_KEYRx) and initialization registers (AES\_IVRx) are not sensitive to the swap mode selection.

#### Data padding

[Figure 206](#XREF_13001__Figure_2__128-bit_block_construction_according_to_the_data_type8) also gives an example of memory data block padding with zeros such that the zeroed bits after the data swap form a contiguous zone at the MSB end of the AES core input buffer. The example shows the padding of an input data block containing:

- 48 message bits, with DATATYPE[1:0] = 01
- 56 message bits, with DATATYPE[1:0] = 10
- 34 message bits, with DATATYPE[1:0] = 11

### 27.4.14 AES key registers

The AES\_KEYRx registers store the encryption or decryption key bitfield KEY[127:0] or KEY[255:0]. The data to write to or to read from each register is organized in the memory in little-endian order, that is, with most significant byte on the highest address.

The key is spread over eight registers as shown in [Table 217](#XREF_15903__Table_11__Key_registers_endianness8).

data-conditional="_tinyaes_sks" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyC" style="margin-top : 0.00pt;margin-bottom : 0.00pt;line-height : 8.00pt;font-size : 7.00pt;">TAMP_BKP5R<br/>[31:0]</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_sks" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyC" style="margin-top : 0.00pt;margin-bottom : 0.00pt;line-height : 8.00pt;font-size 8.00pt;font-size : 7.00pt;">TAMP_BKP3R<br/>[31:0]</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_sks" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyC" style="margin-top : 0.00pt;margin-bottom : 0.00pt;line-height The key for encryption or decryption may be written into these registers when the AES peripheral is disabled, by clearing the EN bit of the AES\_CR register.

The key registers are not affected by the data swapping controlled by DATATYPE[1:0] bitfield of the AES\_CR register.

### 27.4.15 AES initialization vector registers

The four AES\_IVRx registers keep the initialization vector input bitfield IVI[127:0]. The data to write to or to read from each register is organized in the memory in little-endian order, that is, with most significant byte on the highest address. The registers are also ordered from lowest address (AES\_IVR0) to highest address (AES\_IVR3).

The signification of data in the bitfield depends on the chaining mode selected. When used, the bitfield is updated upon each computation cycle of the AES core.

Write operations to the AES\_IVRx registers when the AES peripheral is enabled have no effect to the register contents. For modifying the contents of the AES\_IVRx registers, the EN bit of the AES\_CR register must first be cleared.

Reading the AES\_IVRx registers returns the latest counter value (useful for managing suspend mode).

The AES\_IVRx registers are not affected by the data swapping feature controlled by the DATATYPE[1:0] bitfield of the AES\_CR register.

<a id="XREF_62006__1_3_16_AES_DMA_interface8"></a>

### 27.4.16 AES DMA interface

The AES peripheral provides an interface to connect to the DMA (direct memory access) controller. The DMA operation is controlled through the AES\_CR register.

#### Data input using DMA

Setting the DMAINEN bit of the AES\_CR register enables DMA writing into AES. The AES peripheral then initiates a DMA request during the input phase each time it requires to write a 128-bit block (quadruple word) to the AES\_DINR register, as shown in [Figure 207](#XREF_61428__Figure_33__DMA_requests_and_data_transfers_during_Input_phase8).

**Note:**

According to the algorithm and the mode selected, special padding / ciphertext stealing might be required. For example, in case of AES GCM encryption or AES CCM decryption, a DMA transfer must not include the last block. For details, refer to [Section 27.4.4: AES procedure to perform a cipher operation](#XREF_62827__1_3_4_AES_procedure_to_perform_a_cipher_operation8).

<a id="XREF_61428__Figure_33__DMA_requests_and_data_transfers_during_Input_phase8"></a>

#### Figure 207. DMA transfer of a 128-bit data block during input phase![](MSv42160V1.svg)

#### Data output using DMA

Setting the DMAOUTEN bit of the AES\_CR register enables DMA reading from AES. The AES peripheral then initiates a DMA request during the Output phase each time it requires to read a 128-bit block (quadruple word) to the AES\_DINR register, as shown in [Figure 208](#XREF_67175__Figure_8__DMA_requests_during_Output_phase_(AES_OUT)8).

**Note:**

According to the message size, extra bytes might need to be discarded by application in the last block.

<a id="XREF_67175__Figure_8__DMA_requests_during_Output_phase_(AES_OUT)8"></a>

#### Figure 208. DMA transfer of a 128-bit data block during output phase![](MSv42161V1.svg)

#### DMA operation in different operating modes

DMA operations are usable when Mode 1 (encryption) or Mode 3 (decryption) are selected via the MODE[1:0] bitfield of the register AES\_CR. As in Mode 2 (key derivation) the AES\_KEYRx registers must be written by software, enabling the DMA transfer through the DMAINEN and DMAOUTEN bits of the AES\_CR register have no effect in that mode.

DMA single requests are generated by AES until it is disabled. So, after the data output phase at the end of processing of a 128-bit data block, AES switches automatically to a new data input phase for the next data block, if any.

When the data transferring between AES and memory is managed by DMA, the CCF flag has no use because the reading of the AES\_DOUTR register is managed by DMA automatically at the end of the computation phase. The CCF flag must only be cleared when transiting back to data transferring managed by software. See [Section 27.4.4: AES procedure to perform a cipher operation](#XREF_62827__1_3_4_AES_procedure_to_perform_a_cipher_operation8), subsection [Data append](#XREF_12104__Data_append8), for details.

### 27.4.17 AES error management

AES configuration can be changed at any moment by clearing the EN bit of the AES\_CR register.

#### Read error flag (RDERR)

Unexpected read attempt of the AES\_DOUTR register sets the RDERR flag of the AES\_SR register, and returns zero.

RDERR is triggered during the computation phase or during the input phase.

**Note:**

AES is not disabled upon a RDERR error detection and continues processing.

An interrupt is generated if the ERRIE bit of the AES\_CR register is set. For more details, refer to [Section 27.5: AES interrupts](#XREF_33257__1_4_AES_interrupts8).

The RDERR flag is cleared by setting the ERRIE bit of the AES\_CR register.

#### Write error flag (WDERR)

Unexpected write attempt of the AES\_DINR register sets the WRERR flag of the AES\_SR register, and has no effect on the AES\_DINR register. The WRERR is triggered during the computation phase or during the output phase.

**Note:**

AES is not disabled after a WRERR error detection and continues processing.

An interrupt is generated if the ERRIE bit of the AES\_CR register is set. For more details, refer to [Section 27.5: AES interrupts](#XREF_33257__1_4_AES_interrupts8).

The WRERR flag is cleared by setting the ERRC bit of the AES\_CR register.

<a id="XREF_33257__1_4_AES_interrupts8"></a>

## 27.5 AES interrupts

Individual maskable interrupt sources generated by the AES peripheral signal the following events:

- computation completed
- read error
- write error

These sources are combined into a common interrupt signal from the AES peripheral that connects to the Arm® Cortex® interrupt controller. Each can individually be enabled/disabled, by setting/clearing the corresponding enable bit of the AES\_CR register, and cleared by setting the corresponding bit of the AES\_CR register.

The status of each can be read from the AES\_SR register.

[Table 218](#XREF_15465__Table_11__AES_interrupt_requests8) gives a summary of the interrupt sources, their event flags and enable bits.

style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;display: style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;display: <div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="76.51px"/><col width="538.08px"/><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_newmodel" height="85.33px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_newmodel" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 31</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_newmodel" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">IPRST</span>: <span class="Bold" data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">AES</span> peripheral software reset</p><p class="TableIndent">Setting the bit resets the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> peripheral, putting all registers to their default values, except the IPRST bit itself. Hence, any key-relative data is lost. For this reason, it is recommended to set the bit before handing over the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> to a less secure application.</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit must be low while writing any configuration registers.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel,_notinyaes_saes" height="22.67px"><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel,_notinyaes_saes"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits <span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel,_notinyaes_saes">31</span>:<span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel,_notinyaes_saes">24</span></p></td><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel,_notinyaes_saes"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_sks" height="396.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_sks" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 30:28</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_sks" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">KEYSEL[2:0]</span>: Key selection</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield defines the source of the key information to use in the AES cryptographic core.</p><p class="TableIndent">000: Software key, loaded in key registers <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_KEYx</p><p class="TableIndent">001: Derived hardware unique key (DHUK)</p><p class="TableIndent">010: Boot hardware key (BHK)</p><p class="TableIndent">100: XOR of DHUK and BHK</p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_saes,_tinyaes_sks">Others: Test mode key (256-bit hardware constant 0xA5A5...A5A5)</span></p><p class="TableIndent">When KEYSEL is different from zero, selected key value is available in key registers when BUSY bit is cleared and KEYVALID is set in the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_SR register. Otherwise, the key error flag KEIF is set. Repeated writing of KEYSEL[2:0] with the same non-zero value only triggers the loading of DHUK or BHK if KEYVALID = 0.</p><p class="TableIndent">When the application software changes the key selection by writing the KEYSEL[2:0] bitfield, the key registers are immediately erased and the KEYVALID flag cleared.</p><p class="TableIndent">At the end of the decryption process, if KMOD[1:0] is other than zero, KEYSEL[2:0] is cleared.</p><p class="TableIndent">Attempts to write the bitfield are ignored when the EN bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register is set before the write access and it is not cleared by that write access.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_dualkshare,_tinyaes_singlekshare" height="148.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_dualkshare,_tinyaes_singlekshare" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 27:26</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_dualkshare,_tinyaes_singlekshare" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">KSHAREID[1:0]</span>: Key share identification</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield defines, at the end of a decryption process with KMOD[1:0] = 10 (shared key), which target can read the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> key registers using a dedicated hardware bus.</p><p class="TableIndent">00: <span data-keyref="~tinyaes_kshareTarget1">N/A</span> peripheral</p><p class="TableIndent">Others: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">Attempts to write the bitfield are ignored when the BUSY flag of <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_SR register is set, as well as when the EN bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register is set before the write access and it is not cleared by that write access.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_dualkshare,_tinyaes_kshareaes,_tinyaes_singlekshare,_tinyaes_wrapk" height="392.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_dualkshare,_tinyaes_kshareaes,_tinyaes_singlekshare,_tinyaes_wrapk" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 25:24</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_dualkshare,_tinyaes_kshareaes,_tinyaes_singlekshare,_tinyaes_wrapk" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">KMOD[1:0]</span>: Key mode selection</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield defines how the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> key can be used by the application:</p><p class="TableIndent">00: Normal key</p><p class="TableIndent">Others: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">With normal key selection, the key registers are freely usable, no specific usage or protection applies to <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_DIN and <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_DOUT registers.</p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-keyref="~tinyaes_kmod_ins1"></span>Attempts to write the bitfield are ignored when the EN bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register is set before the write access and it is not cleared by that write access.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm" height="104.00px"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 23:20</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NPBLB[3:0]: </span>Number of padding bytes in last block</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield sets the number of padding bytes in last block of payload:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000: All bytes are valid (no padding)</p><p class="TableIndent">0001: Padding for one least-significant byte of last block</p><p class="TableIndent">...</p><p class="TableIndent">1111: Padding for 15 least-significant bytes of last block</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_notinyaes_sks,_notinyaes_tz_sks,_notinyaes_xcm" height="22.67px"><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_sks,_notinyaes_tz_sks,_notinyaes_xcm"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit <span data-conditional="_notinyaes_sks,_notinyaes_tz_sks,_notinyaes_xcm">19</span></p></td><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_sks,_notinyaes_tz_sks,_notinyaes_xcm"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_tz_sks" height="205.33px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_tz_sks" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_tz_sks" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">KEYPROT</span>: Key protection</p><p class="TableIndent">When set, hardware-based key protection is enabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: When KEYVALID is set and KEYSEL = 0 application can transfer the ownership of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>, with its loaded key, to an application running in another security context (such as nonsecure, secure).</p><p class="TableIndent">1: When KEYVALID is set, key error flag (KEIF) is set when an access to any registers is detected, this access having a security context (for example, secure, nonsecure) that does not match the one of the application that loaded the key.</p><p class="TableIndent">Attempts to write the bit are ignored when the EN bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register is set before the write access and it is not cleared by that write access.</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">KEYSIZE: </span>Key size selection</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield defines the length of the key used in the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> cryptographic core, in bits:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: 128</p><p class="TableIndent">1: 256</p><p class="TableIndent">Attempts to write the bit are ignored when the EN bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register is set before the write access and it is not cleared by that write access.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_notinyaes_xcm" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_xcm" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_xcm" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm" height="134.67px"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 14:13</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">GCMPH[1:0]:</span> GCM or CCM phase selection</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield selects the phase of GCM, GMAC or CCM algorithm:</p><p class="TableIndent">00: Init phase</p><p class="TableIndent">01: Header phase</p><p class="TableIndent">10: Payload phase</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Final phase</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield has no effect if other than GCM, GMAC or CCM algorithms are selected (through the ALGOMODE bitfield).</p></td></tr><tr height="133.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">DMAOUTEN</span>: DMA output enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables/disables data transferring with DMA, in the output phase:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Enable</p><p class="TableIndent">When the bit is set, DMA requests are automatically generated by <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> during the output data phase. This feature is only effective when Mode 1 or Mode 3 is selected through the MODE[1:0] bitfield. It is not effective for Mode 2 (key derivation).</p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4">Use of DMA with Mode 4 (single decryption) is not recommended.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="133.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;">DMAINEN<span style="font-weight:normal;">:</span> <span style="font-weight:normal;">DMA input enable</span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables/disables data transferring with DMA, in the input phase:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Enable</p><p class="TableIndent">When the bit is set, DMA requests are automatically generated by <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> during the input data phase. This feature is only effective when Mode 1 or Mode 3 is selected through the MODE[1:0] bitfield. It is not effective for Mode 2 (key derivation).</p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4">Use of DMA with Mode 4 (single decryption) is not recommended.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits" height="86.67px"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ERRIE</span>: Error interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables or disables (masks) the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> interrupt generation when RDERR and/or WRERR is set:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable (mask)</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Enable</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits" height="86.67px"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CCFIE</span>: CCF interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables or disables (masks) the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> interrupt generation when CCF (computation complete flag) is set:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable (mask)</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Enable</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits" height="88.00px"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ERRC</span>: Error flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Upon written to 1, this bit clears the RDERR and WRERR error flags in the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_SR register:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No effect</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Clear RDERR and WRERR flags</p><p class="TableIndent">Reading the flag always returns zero.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits" height="88.00px"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits"><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"><span class="Bold" data-keyref="~tinyaes_CCFclbit" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">CCFC</span>:</span> Computation complete flag clear</p><p class="TableIndent">Upon written to 1, this bit clears the computation complete flag (CCF) in the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_SR register:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No effect</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Clear CCF</p><p class="TableIndent">Reading the flag always returns zero.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_notinyaes_legacybits" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_legacybits" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 10:7</p></td><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_legacybits" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_notinyaes_ctrorxcm" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_ctrorxcm" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_ctrorxcm" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="166.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 16, 6:5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CHMOD[2:0]</span>: Chaining mode selection</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield selects the AES chaining mode:</p><p class="TableIndent">000: Electronic codebook (ECB)</p><p class="TableIndent">001: Cipher-block chaining (CBC)</p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_ctr">010: Counter mode (CTR)</span></p><p class="TableIndent" style="margin-top : 0.00pt;margin-bottom : 0.00pt;"><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm">011: Galois counter mode (GCM) and Galois message authentication code (GMAC)</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm">100: Counter with CBC-MAC (CCM)</span></p><p class="TableIndent">others: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">Attempts to write the bitfield are ignored when the EN bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register is set before the write access and it is not cleared by that write access.</p></td></tr><tr height="164.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 4:3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MODE[1:0]</span>: <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> operating mode</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield selects the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> operating mode:</p><p class="TableIndent">00: Mode 1: encryption</p><p class="TableIndent">01: Mode 2: key derivation (or key preparation for ECB/CBC decryption)</p><p class="TableIndent">10: Mode 3: decryption</p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4">11: Mode 4: key derivation then single decryption</span></p><p class="TableIndent">Attempts to write the bitfield are ignored when the EN bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register is set before the write access and it is not cleared by that write access.<span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4"> Any attempt to selecting Mode 4 while either ECB or CBC chaining mode is not selected, defaults to effective selection of Mode 3. It is not </span><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4">possible to select a Mode 3 following a Mode 4.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="165.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 2:1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DATATYPE[1:0]</span>: Data type selection</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield defines the format of data written in the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_DINR register or read from the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_DOUTR register, through selecting the mode of data swapping:</p><p class="TableIndent">00: None</p><p class="TableIndent">01: Half-word (16-bit)</p><p class="TableIndent">10: Byte (8-bit)</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Bit</p><p class="TableIndent">For more details, refer to <a href="#XREF_71598__1_3_13_AES_data_registers_and_data_swapping8" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 27.4.13: AES data registers and data swapping</a>.</p><p class="TableIndent">Attempts to write the bitfield are ignored when the EN bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register is set before the write access and it is not cleared by that write access.</p></td></tr><tr height="118.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">EN</span>: <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables/disables the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> peripheral:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Enable</p><p class="TableIndent">At any moment, clearing then setting the bit re-initializes the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> peripheral.</p><p class="TableIndent" style="font-style:Italic;"><span style="font-style:normal;">This bit is automatically cleared by hardware upon the completion of the key preparation (Mode 2) </span><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm" style="font-style:normal;">and upon the completion of GCM/GMAC/CCM initial phase</span><span style="font-style:normal;">.</span></p></td></tr></table></div>


### 27.7.2 AES status register (AES\_SR)

Address offset: 0x04

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

width="39.47px"/><col width="42.81px"/><col width="42.90px"/><col width="33.94px"/><col width="43.14px"/><col width="39.14px"/><col width="39.14px"/><col width="42.57px"/><col width="39.47px"/><col width="39.48px"/><tr height="17.33px"><td style="border-bottom-width:0;border-bottom-style:hidden;border-bottom-color:0;"><p class="RegTBodyC">31</p></td><td <div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="76.51px"/><col width="538.08px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:<span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel">4</span></p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_newmodel" height="224.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_newmodel" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_newmodel" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">KEYVALID</span>: Key Valid flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the amount of key information defined by KEYSIZE in <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR has been loaded in <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_KEYx key registers.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No valid key information is available in key registers. EN bit in <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR cannot be set.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Valid key information, defined by KEYSIZE in <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR, is loaded in key registers.</p><p class="TableIndent">In normal mode when KEYSEL equals to zero, the application must write the key registers in the correct sequence, otherwise the KEIF flag of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_ISR register is set and KEYVALID stays at zero.</p><p class="TableIndent">When KEYSEL is different from zero the BUSY flag is automatically set by <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>. When key is loaded successfully, the BUSY flag is cleared and KEYVALID set. Upon an error, the KEIF flag of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_ISR register is set, the BUSY flag cleared and KEYVALID kept at zero.</p><p class="TableIndent">When the KEIF flag is set, the application must clear it through the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_ICR register, otherwise KEYVALID cannot be set. See the KEIF bit description for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">For more information on key loading, refer to <a href="#XREF_73082__1_3_13_AES_key_registers8" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 27.4.14: AES key registers</a>.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_newmodel" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_newmodel" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 6:4</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_newmodel" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_notinyaes_saes" height="133.33px"><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_saes"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td data-conditional="_notinyaes_saes"><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;">BUSY: <span style="font-weight:normal;">Busy</span></p><p class="TableIndent">This flag indicates whether <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> is idle or busy during GCM payload <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">encryption</span> phase:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Idle</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Busy</p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_saes,_tinyaes_xcm">When the flag indicates “idle”, the current GCM encryption processing may be suspended to process a higher-priority message. In other chaining modes, or in GCM phases other than payload </span><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_saes,_tinyaes_xcm">encryption, the flag must be ignored for the suspend process.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_tinyaes_saes" height="118.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_saes" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td data-conditional="_tinyaes_saes" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;">BUSY: <span style="font-weight:normal;">Busy</span></p><p class="TableIndent">This flag indicates whether <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> is idle or busy during GCM payload <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">encryption</span> phase:</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Idle</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Busy</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag is also set upon <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> initialization, upon fetching random number from the RNG, or upon transferring a shared key to a target peripheral.</p></td></tr><tr height="164.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WRERR</span>: Write error</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag indicates the detection of an unexpected write operation to the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_DINR register (during computation or data output phase): </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Not detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Detected</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag is set by hardware. It is cleared by software upon setting the <span data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits">ERRC</span> bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_<span data-keyref="~tinyaes_Intclreg">CR</span> register.</p><p class="TableIndent">Upon the flag setting, an interrupt is generated if enabled through the <span data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits">ERRIE</span> bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_<span data-keyref="~tinyaes_Intclreg">CR</span> register.</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag setting has no impact on the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> operation. Unexpected write is ignored.</p></td></tr><tr height="164.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RDERR</span>: Read error flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag indicates the detection of an unexpected read operation from the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_DOUTR register (during computation or data input phase): </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Not detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Detected</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag is set by hardware. It is cleared by software upon setting the <span data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits">ERRC</span> bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_<span data-keyref="~tinyaes_Intclreg">CR</span> register.</p><p class="TableIndent">Upon the flag setting, an interrupt is generated if enabled through the <span data-conditional="_tinyaes_legacybits">ERRIE</span> bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_<span data-keyref="~tinyaes_Intclreg">CR</span> register.</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag setting has no impact on the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> operation. Unexpected read returns zero.</p></td></tr><tr height="149.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CCF</span>: Computation completed flag</p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel">This flag indicates whether the computation is completed:</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel">0: Not completed</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel">1: Completed</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel">The flag is set by hardware upon the completion of the computation. It is cleared by software, upon setting the <span data-keyref="~tinyaes_CCFclbit">CCFC</span> bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_<span data-keyref="~tinyaes_Intclreg">CR</span> register.</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel">Upon the flag setting, an interrupt is generated if enabled through the CCFIE bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register.</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_newmodel">The flag is significant only when the DMAOUTEN bit is 0. It may stay high when DMA_EN is 1.</span></p></td></tr></table></div>


### 27.7.3 AES data input register (AES\_DINR)

Address offset: 0x08

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Only 32-bit access type is supported.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="76.51px"/><col width="538.08px"/><tr height="193.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DIN[31:0]</span>: Input data word</p><p class="TableIndent">A four-fold sequential write to this bitfield during the input phase results in writing a complete 128-bit block of input data to the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> peripheral. From the first to the fourth write, the corresponding data weights are [127:96], [95:64], [63:32], and [31:0]. Upon each write, the data from the 32-bit input buffer are handled by the data swap block according to the DATATYPE[1:0] bitfield, then written into the AES core 128-bit input buffer.</p><p class="TableIndent">The data signification of the input data block depends on the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> operating mode:</p><p class="TableIndent">- <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 1</span> (encryption): plaintext</p><p class="TableIndent">- <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 2</span> (key derivation): the bitfield is not used (<span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_KEYRx registers used for input)</p><p class="TableIndent">- <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 3</span> (decryption)<span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4"> and </span><span class="Bold" data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4" style="font-family : Arial ; ### 27.7.4 AES data output register (AES\_DOUTR)

Address offset: 0x0C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Only 32-bit read access type is supported.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="76.51px"/><col width="538.08px"/><tr height="194.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DOUT[31:0]</span>: Output data word</p><p class="TableIndent">This read-only bitfield fetches a 32-bit output buffer. A four-fold sequential read of this bitfield, upon the computation completion (CCF set), virtually reads a complete 128-bit block of output data from the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> peripheral. Before reaching the output buffer, the data produced by the AES core are handled by the data swap block according to the DATATYPE[1:0] bitfield.</p><p class="TableIndent">Data weights from the first to the fourth read operation are: [127:96], [95:64], [63:32], and [31:0].</p><p class="TableIndent">The data signification of the output data block depends on the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> operating mode:</p><p class="TableIndent">- <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 1</span> (encryption): ciphertext</p><p class="TableIndent">- <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 2</span> (key derivation): the bitfield is not used<span data-conditional="_notinyaes_kwriteonly"> (<span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_KEYRx registers used for output)</span></p><p class="TableIndent">- <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 3</span> (decryption)<span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4"> and </span><span class="Bold" data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 4</span><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4"> (key derivation then single decryption)</span>: plaintext</p><p class="TableIndent">The data swap operation is described in <a href="#XREF_71598__1_3_13_AES_data_registers_and_data_swapping8" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 27.4.13: AES data registers and data swapping on page 863</a>.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 27.7.5 AES key register 0 (AES\_KEYR0)

Address offset: 0x10

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="76.51px"/><col width="538.08px"/><tr height="240.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">KEY[31:0]</span>: Cryptographic key, bits [31:0]</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield contains the bits [31:0] of the AES encryption or decryption key, depending on the operating mode:</p><p class="TableIndent">- In <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 1</span> (encryption), <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 2</span> (key derivation)<span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4"> and </span><span class="Bold" data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 4</span><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4"> (key derivation then single decryption)</span>: the value to write into the bitfield is the encryption key.</p><p class="TableIndent">- In <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mode 3</span> (decryption): the value to write into the bitfield is the encryption key to be derived before being used for decryption.<span data-conditional="_notinyaes_kwriteonly"> After writing the encryption key into the bitfield, its reading before enabling <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> returns the same value. Its reading after enabling <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> and after the CCF flag is set </span><span data-conditional="_notinyaes_kwriteonly">returns the decryption key derived from the encryption key.</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_tinyaes_mode4">In mode 4 (key derivation then single decryption) the bitfield always contains the encryption key.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">The <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_KEYRx registers may be written only when KEYSIZE value is correct and when the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span> peripheral is disabled (EN bit of the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_CR register cleared).  Note that, if, the key is directly loaded to <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_KEYRx registers (hence writes to key register is ignored and KEIF is set).</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_73082__1_3_13_AES_key_registers8" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 27.4.14: AES key registers on page 865</a> for more details.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 27.7.6 AES key register 1 (AES\_KEYR1)

Address offset: 0x14

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

### 27.7.10 AES initialization vector register 1 (AES\_IVR1)

Address offset: 0x24

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

### 27.7.14 AES key register 5 (AES\_KEYR5)

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="76.51px"/><col width="538.08px"/><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">KEY[255:224]</span>: Cryptographic key, bits [255:224]</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to the <span data-keyref="~tiny_aes3_IPACRONYM">AES</span>_KEYR0 register for description of the KEY[255:0] bitfield.</p></td></tr></table></div>


**Note:**

The key registers from 4 to 7 are used only when the key length of 256 bits is selected. They have no effect when the key length of 128 bits is selected (only key registers 0 to 3 are used in that case).

### 27.7.17 AES suspend registers (AES\_SUSPxR)![](RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_tiny_aes3_UserSpec_FOM_SVG_493.svg)

Address offset: 0x040 + 0x4 \* x, (x = 0 to 7)

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

These registers contain the complete internal register states of the AES processor when the AES processing of the current task is suspended to process a higher-priority task.

Upon suspend, the software reads and saves the AES\_SUSPxR register contents (where x is from 0 to 7) into memory, before using the AES processor for the higher-priority task. Upon completion, the software restores the saved contents back into the corresponding suspend registers, before resuming the original task.


<div align="left" data-conditional="_tinyaes_xcm"><table class="_Invisible"><col width="616.08px"/><tr height="24.00px"><td><p class="Note">Note:<span style="padding-right:33pt ;">​</span>These registers are used only when<span data-conditional="_notinyaes_saes,_tinyaes_xcm"> GCM, GMAC, or</span> CCM chaining mode is selected.</p></td></tr></table></div>


These registers can be read only when AES is enabled. Reading these registers while AES is disabled returns 0x0000 0000.
